4:T15e6,## Eliza Cloud Apps (Product 2) — scaling + ingress gaps to close before go-live

A code-level audit of the apps data plane (per-tenant DB + isolated container deploys). **The core design is sound and standard** — per-tenant `DATABASE`+`ROLE` with `REVOKE CONNECT` (real cross-tenant isolation), per-app `--internal` network + DB ambassador, `ClusterPool` sharding. This is *not* jank. But the current code's realistic ceiling is **~dozens of concurrently-running apps, not ~1000**, and the front-door router isn't installed yet. Filing the prioritized gaps (all evidenced) so they're tracked before flipping `APPS_DEPLOY_ENABLED` in prod.

Context: terraform apply is also currently blocked on the staging Hetzner **server limit** (see #8318). Apply bugs (SSH-key 409, retired `cpx41`) are fixed in #8318.

### 🔴 P0 — pre-launch blockers (serve ~0 reachable / isolation maybe inactive)
1. **Ingress not installed.** `cloud-init/app-node.yaml.tftpl:62-63` leaves Caddy as a `STAN:` TODO — only squid is installed. No per-app `<id>.apps.elizacloud.ai` hostname routes to any container (nothing terminates TLS / reverse-proxies 80/443 → the random high host port). **Serves 0 reachable users until added.** (config)
2. **Postgres connection ceiling.** `cloud-init/tenant-db.yaml.tftpl:12` leaves `max_connections` at the default 100, and there's **no pgbouncer** anywhere; each app's plugin-sql pool is `max:20 / min:2` (`plugins/plugin-sql/src/pg/manager.ts:16-17`). Exhausted at ~5 apps under load / ~50 idle — `ClusterPool` shards DB *count* (2000 cap), not connections. (config + a pooler)
3. **Env-var contract unverified.** Orchestrator injects `DATABASE_URL` (`app-deploy-orchestrator.ts:82`) but plugin-sql's primary path reads `POSTGRES_URL`. The cloud eliza loaders *do* map `DATABASE_URL→POSTGRES_URL` (agent.ts:10, settings.ts:100, …), so it's satisfiable — but must be confirmed for the actual app image, else the isolated DB is provisioned and never used (silent PGlite fallback) → per-tenant isolation isn't even active. (code/verify)

### 🟠 P1 — required before real scale (multi-node)
4. **App placement is single-node.** `container-executor-deps.ts:42-52` always picks `seed.split(',')[0]` — every container piles on node 1; extra terraform app nodes get no placements. No `node_id`/`incrementAllocated` for app containers (`app-container-store.ts`). (code)
5. **Docker network exhaustion.** One `--internal` bridge per app exhausts the default 172.x address pool at ~31 networks/node; `daemon.json` `default-address-pools` not set in cloud-init. Caps a node at a few dozen tenants regardless of CPU/RAM. (config)
6. **Multi-node ingress LB.** Wildcard DNS hard-codes `app_node["1"]` (`main.tf:184,190`); no `hcloud_load_balancer`. The moment `app_node_count>1`, apps on node 2+ are dark. (config)
7. **Tenant-DB slot leak.** `tenant_db_clusters.database_count` only increments (`tenant-db-clusters.ts:44`); no `releaseSlot`/decrement, and `tenant-db-provisioner.deprovision()` (DROP DATABASE/ROLE) has **zero non-test callers** — `UserDatabaseService.cleanupDatabase` only deletes Neon. Under churn, slots ratchet up + orphan DBs accumulate. (code)
8. **Host-port collisions.** `allocateHostPort()` is a blind random pick in 20000-39999 with no in-use check (`container-executor-deps.ts:86-90`), unlike the agent path's `getUsedDockerHostPorts`. Flakes docker-create at a few hundred containers/node. (code)
9. **App-plane autoscaler.** `node-autoscaler.ts` is agent-image/platform hardcoded (lines 133,233,435); no autoscaler cron; app provisioning never registers allocation → app demand triggers nothing. Adding app capacity is manual. (code)
10. **Billing cron at scale + idempotency.** `cron/container-billing/route.ts:391-440` is one serial Worker pass with an N+1 per-org prefetch + inline emails (presses Worker wall-time in the low thousands; can't resume a cut-off pass), and `recordSuccessfulDailyBilling` (`container-billing.ts:153-212`) has **no unique constraint on (container_id, billing_period)** → a duplicate cron fire / manual re-run double-bills. *(2AM-owned billing surface.)*
11. **Defense-in-depth written but unused.** `buildAppNodeNftablesRules` (drops 169.254.169.254 metadata + RFC1918 pivots), per-app `buildSquidAllowlistConf`, `buildHetznerAppFirewallRules` have **zero non-test callers** — egress is one shared squid + static global allowlist, the metadata/lateral block is code-only, and `-p` binds `0.0.0.0` so app ports are world-exposed until the firewall's applied. (NB: reconcile the nftables RFC1918 drop with the ambassador's legit 10.30.x DB path.) (both)

### 🟡 P2 — later / hardening
12. Single Postgres superuser per cluster = shared fate (rotation + a non-superuser provisioning role); tighten `operator_ingress_cidrs` (SSH `0.0.0.0/0`); real tenant-DB TLS cert (hostssl vs the `sslmode=disable` fallback).
13. Kernel sandboxing decision (gVisor/Kata/userns + seccomp) + per-app `--cpus` (only `--memory`/`--pids-limit` set today). Right-size `max_databases` (2000 is misleading until pooling exists). Tenant-DB-node scaling runbook.

**Honest bottom line:** isolation + sharding are real and correct; the work to reach 1000 is *known* (the code is full of accurate `STAN TODO`s) but **not built**. Recommend **not** going live until at least P0 (and ideally #4/#6) are closed. Happy to take the additive, testable items (e.g. #3 set both env vars, #7 slot release, #8 collision-safe ports) as PRs — flag which you want vs. own.

cc @standujar (data-plane) — billing items (#10) are 2AM's surface.
5:T13a6,Evidence-based audit of the Apps (Product 2) hosting + per-tenant-DB billing. **Bottom line: profitable on the happy path, but 4 leaks must be fixed before go-live.** Nothing is bleeding *today* (the apps lane isn't armed/deployed yet) — these are pre-launch fixes.

## ✅ Profitable on the happy path
- **Hosting:** a default app bills ~**$1.17/day** ($0.67 base × 1.75 CPU multiplier from the schema default `cpu=1792`) vs **~$0.29–0.58/day** real Hetzner cost → **~2–4× gross margin**, break-even ~1 container/node. Apps **are** enrolled in billing (our deploy sets `billing_status='active'` by schema default → 2AM's `container-billing` cron picks them up like agents). Not hosting for free.
- **Inference:** end user pays (`cost = provider_cost × 1.20`); platform never subsidizes; creator/affiliate markups stack **on top** of the user's bill. Always net-positive.

## ⚠️ Leaks / correctness bugs (owner-tagged)

### CRITICAL
- [ ] **[ours] Suspended containers are never `docker stop`ped.** `suspendContainer()` only flips DB flags (`container-billing.ts:117-126`); the node container runs `--restart unless-stopped` (`client.ts:249`) and no reconciler stops `billing_status='suspended'` rows. After suspend the row drops out of `listBillableContainers` forever → a deadbeat's app **runs indefinitely on Hetzner, billed never** (~$210/yr, unbounded). *Fix:* on the shutdown branch, SSH `docker stop` (via our provider) + decrement node allocation, and add a reconciler. (Cross-ref: `suspendContainer` is 2AM's; the stop is ours.)
- [ ] **[ours] Deleted isolated-DB apps never drop their Postgres + never release the cluster slot.** `SqlTenantDbProvisioner.deprovision` exists (`tenant-db-provisioner.ts:166`) but has **zero live callers**; there is no `releaseSlot` (only `tryClaimSlot` increments). `app-cleanup` drops domains/repo/secrets but not the DB; `cleanupDatabase` handles only the Neon path. → every deleted isolated app **strands a live DB we pay for + burns 1/2000 cluster slots permanently**. *Fix (focused lifecycle PR):* enqueue a deprovision **job** on app delete (DROP DATABASE/ROLE needs `pg` → daemon) + add `releaseSlot` (`database_count = GREATEST(0, database_count-1)`) + extend `cleanupDatabase` for the `direct_pg`/isolated path. **This is the missing half of #8338 (opt-in per-tenant DB).**
- [ ] **[2AM] `container-billing` cron has no idempotency gate.** It writes `last_billed_at`/`next_billing_at` but never reads them (`container-billing.ts:57-81`); a Cloudflare retry or duplicate POST **re-debits every running container a full extra day** → over-charge → chargebacks. *Fix:* port the agent-billing rebill guard + a unique partial index on `(container_id, billing_period)`.

### HIGH
- [ ] **[design — nubs] Per-tenant DB / storage / bandwidth bill $0.** The ccx33 tenant-DB node (~$2.05/day incl. 200GB vol) has **no billing line**; an `isolated` app and a `none` app are billed identically. At low density the apps lane runs **negative** (break-even ~3 paying containers; 1 container = −$1.67/day). *Decision:* (a) add a per-isolated-DB daily line (amortized floor ~$0.035/DB, realistically $1–3/mo) gated on `databaseMode='isolated'`, **or** (b) fold DB cost into the container fee + set a minimum density before provisioning the dedicated ccx33.
- [ ] **[design — nubs] Deploy takes no upfront charge / no balance precheck** (and bypasses the quota check). Grace-window hosting is free; repeatable spin-up/tear-down costs us. *Fix:* charge `DEPLOYMENT` ($0.50) at create, or require `credit_balance ≥ 1–2 days` before provisioning.

### MEDIUM / LOW
- [ ] **[2AM] `organizations.credit_balance` DEFAULTS to `$100`** (`organizations.ts:33-35`) — any future org-create that omits the field grants ~$100 free hosting. *Fix:* default `'0.00'`; grant starter credits explicitly.
- [ ] **[2AM] Daily debit is a non-atomic JS read-modify-write** (absolute balance) → lost-update vs concurrent inference debits/top-ups. *Fix:* route through `creditsService.deductCredits` (relative decrement with a guard).
- [ ] **[ours] No `--cpus` limit** — we bill a CPU tier (1792→1.75×) but enforce only `--memory`; and our app containers actually provision at `cpu:1/512MB` (`APP_CONTAINER_DEFAULTS`) while billing assumes 1792/1792. *Fix:* reconcile the provision defaults with the billed dimensions + add `--cpus`.
- [ ] **[design] Copy says $0.67/day; real default is $1.17/day**; cost-basis constants are AWS references, not Hetzner. Reconcile copy / cost basis.

## Numbers
- Revenue: $0.67/day base ×1.75 (cpu=1792) = **$1.17/day**. Real Hetzner: ccx23 ~$0.79/day (app node), ccx33 ~$1.74/day + 200GB ~$0.31/day (tenant DB). Container margin ~2–4×; full-lane break-even ~3 containers.
- Inference: `cost_to_user = provider_cost × 1.20`, always positive.

Audit method: 6-agent read-only sweep of pricing constants, the container-billing cron + repo, the tenant-db provisioning/cluster code, the apps deploy path, and the inference billing path.6:Tda8,## Symptom
`bun run --cwd packages/app build:android` from a clean tree produces an APK with `assets/agent` but **zero `assets/public`, no `capacitor.config.json`** → on device the WebView shows `https://localhost/ net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` forever (no Capacitor `errorPath` is configured, so there's no retry). Verified on a Solana Seeker tonight.

## Root cause
`7ac0e1fb96` (fix(rv64-pipeline), May 20) moved `androidDir` in `packages/app-core/scripts/run-mobile-build.mjs:89` from `packages/app/android` → `packages/app-core/platforms/android` (gradle, native patches, agent staging) — but `runCapacitor(["sync","android"])` still runs with cwd `packages/app` and `capacitor.config.ts` has **no `android.path`**, so the Capacitor CLI keeps delivering the web layer to `packages/app/android`. Nothing bridges the two trees: `capacitor-platform-templates.mjs:27-43` explicitly skips `assets/public`, `assets/agent`, and the generated capacitor JSONs when materializing templates.

Evidence it used to be one project: the tracked `packages/app-core/platforms/android/capacitor.settings.gradle` projectDirs (`../../../app/node_modules/…`) are only producible with the platform dir at `app-core/platforms/android` — i.e. an `android.path` override existed and was lost. A pre-May-20 develop worktree (`bb69a3642e`) has all five asset trees together under `packages/app/android`. Same asymmetry: `iosDir` lives in the host app (`packages/app/ios/App`), android was left behind.

## Why CI doesn't catch it
- `build-android.yml` / `mobile-build-smoke.yml` only assert the APK **file exists** (smoke: lines 255-262); the artifact audits (`auditAndroidCloudArtifact`) check banned entries only, never that `assets/public/index.html` is present.
- No workflow boots the app; `mobile-local-chat-smoke.mjs` isn't wired into any workflow.
- Builds on machines with stale untracked `assets/public` leftovers in the platforms tree appear to work — clean checkouts never do.

## Verified workaround (tonight, on-device confirmed)
After `build:android`: copy `packages/app/android/app/src/main/assets/{public,capacitor.config.json,capacitor.plugins.json}` + `res/xml/config.xml` into `packages/app-core/platforms/android/app/src/main/...`, re-run `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug` there → complete APK (282→336MB), app boots to onboarding on a real device. Caveat: the tracked canonical `capacitor.settings.gradle` lags the synced one (missing background-runner, barcode-scanner, browser, device, haptics, network plugin projects → runtime "plugin not implemented" degradation).

## Proposed fix (either)
1. Restore the lost wiring: `android: { path: "../app-core/platforms/android" }` in `packages/app/capacitor.config.ts` (and teach `ensure-capacitor-platform.mjs`/`isCapacitorPlatformReady` to probe that dir), so sync/copy/update land directly in the gradle tree; or
2. Add a `stageAndroidWebAssets()` step after every `runCapacitor(["sync","android"])` call (4 android targets) copying the web payload + regenerated capacitor gradle files (with path rewrite) into `androidDir`.

Plus, regardless: a **positive APK audit** for sideload targets (assert `assets/public/index.html` + `assets/capacitor.config.json` + `assets/agent/` via `jar tf`) and the same one-liner in CI after assembleDebug, so a web-less APK can never ship silently again.

Found while device-testing the PR #8340 APK; happy to PR whichever shape you prefer — flagging first since the mobile pipeline is active WIP.7:Tf28,## Summary

After working around #8000 by installing `@elizaos/cli` directly (`bun add -g @elizaos/cli`), the CLI still crashes at boot because `engine.io` (transitive) calls `(0, debug_1.default)("engine:transport")` and `debug_1.default` is `undefined` under Bun's CJS resolver. The boot path eagerly loads `engine.io` even for commands that do not need a server (`create`, `--version`, etc).

## Repro

```bash
bun add -g @elizaos/cli      # installs @elizaos/cli@1.7.2 (npm latest)
elizaos create my-app --template project
```

```
3 | exports.Transport = void 0;
4 | const events_1 = require("events");
5 | const parser_v4 = require("engine.io-parser");
6 | const parser_v3 = require("./parser-v3/index");
7 | const debug_1 = require("debug");
8 | const debug = (0, debug_1.default)("engine:transport");
                       ^
TypeError: (0, debug_1.default) is not a function. (In '(0, debug_1.default)("engine:transport")',
'(0, debug_1.default)' is undefined)
      at /Users/<u>/.bun/install/global/node_modules/engine.io/build/transport.js:8:27
      at /Users/<u>/.bun/install/global/node_modules/engine.io/build/transports/polling.js:3:1
      at /Users/<u>/.bun/install/global/node_modules/engine.io/build/transports/index.js:2:1
      at /Users/<u>/.bun/install/global/node_modules/engine.io/build/server.js:431:5
      at /Users/<u>/.bun/install/global/node_modules/engine.io/build/engine.io.js:6:1

Bun v1.3.10 (macOS arm64)
```

## Root cause

`engine.io`'s compiled `transport.js` emits:

```js
const debug_1 = require("debug");
const debug = (0, debug_1.default)("engine:transport");
```

It assumes a `.default` property on the `debug` CJS module. `debug@4.x` exports the factory directly (`module.exports = createDebug`); there is no `.default` property in the CJS shape. Node's CJS-from-ESM interop synthesizes a `default` property automatically; Bun's resolver does not produce the same shape for this dual-package module under the global-bin code path. This is a known engine.io + Bun interop class, not an elizaOS source-level bug.

## Why this is elizaOS's problem in practice

`@elizaos/cli@1.7.2` eagerly loads `engine.io` / Socket.IO at module-init time of *any* command — even `--help` and `create` walk through `engine.io/build/engine.io.js` → `server.js` → `transports/index.js` → `transport.js` before the command's own logic runs. Commands that don't need a websocket server should not be loading the websocket server stack at parse time.

## Environment

- @elizaos/cli@1.7.2 (`npm view @elizaos/cli dist-tags.latest`)
- Node v24.13.0
- Bun v1.3.10
- macOS arm64

## Suggested mitigations (any one would unblock)

1. **Lazy-load `engine.io` / Socket.IO off the boot path.** Move the server imports behind the commands that actually serve (`start`, `dev`). `create`, `info`, `upgrade`, `--version`, `--help` should not transitively touch `engine.io`. This is also a startup-latency win independent of the Bun bug.
2. **Pin / patch the transitive dep.** Either pin `engine.io` to a build whose compiled output uses `__importDefault`, or pin `debug` to a version that exports a named `default` property.
3. **Document Bun support explicitly.** If Bun is a supported runtime for the global CLI, gate publish on a `bun`-runtime smoke (see #8000). If not, the install instructions should say so.

## Status on develop

Verified: develop's `packages/elizaos@2.0.3` scaffolds a project end-to-end via Node (`node packages/elizaos/dist/cli.js create … --template project --yes` succeeds against a fresh `bun install` of the workspace). Either the boot was trimmed or the dep tree was restructured — would be useful for maintainers to surface what changed, since that change is the basis for closing this on the next stable release.

## Workaround

Same as #8000 — use the develop CLI directly until a release supersedes 1.7.2 on `latest`.8:Tc15,## Summary

The current `npm dist-tag latest` for both `elizaos` and `@elizaos/cli` is `1.7.2`. A clean global install fails on the very first command because the `elizaos` alias package's bin shim imports a subpath that `@elizaos/cli@1.7.2`'s `exports` map does not whitelist.

## Repro

```bash
bun add -g elizaos        # or: npm i -g elizaos
elizaos --version         # any subcommand reproduces
```

```
Error [ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED]: Package subpath './dist/index.js' is not defined by "exports" in
/Users/<u>/.bun/install/global/node_modules/@elizaos/cli/package.json
imported from /Users/<u>/.bun/install/global/node_modules/elizaos/bin/elizaos.js
    at exportsNotFound (node:internal/modules/esm/resolve:313:10)
    at packageExportsResolve (node:internal/modules/esm/resolve:661:9)
    ...
  code: 'ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED'
```

## Root cause (verified from npm registry)

`elizaos@1.7.2` ships `bin/elizaos.js`:

```js
import '@elizaos/cli/dist/index.js';
```

`@elizaos/cli@1.7.2` declares:

```json
"exports": {
  ".": "./dist/index.js",
  "./package.json": "./package.json"
}
```

Node's ESM resolver enforces `exports` strictly — `./dist/index.js` as a subpath is blocked even though the file exists. The shim should import the package root (`'@elizaos/cli'`), not a subpath.

## Environment

- elizaos@1.7.2 (`latest` tag)
- @elizaos/cli@1.7.2 (`latest` tag)
- Node v24.13.0
- Bun v1.3.10
- macOS arm64

Confirmed from npm registry:

```
$ npm view elizaos dist-tags
{ latest: '1.7.2', next: '2.0.0-alpha.32', alpha: '2.0.0-alpha.535', beta: '2.0.0-beta.5' }

$ npm view @elizaos/cli dist-tags
{ beta: '1.7.2-beta.0', latest: '1.7.2', alpha: '1.7.3-alpha.4' }
```

## Status on develop

Develop has already restructured: `packages/elizaos` is the CLI itself at v2.0.3; no alias shim, no inter-package subpath import. `@elizaos/cli@1.7.3-alpha.4` also no longer has a separate shim — its `bin` points directly to `dist/index.js`. The structural fix is in; the gap is that no release has superseded the broken `latest` tag.

## What would prevent regression

A pre-publish global-install smoke test:

1. `npm pack` → tarball
2. `npm i -g <tarball>` into a sandboxed prefix
3. Run `<prefix>/bin/elizaos --version` under **both** Node and Bun
4. Assert exit code 0

The existing `packages/elizaos/scripts/packaged-smoke.mjs` does a local install but not a global one — global install resolves bin via a different path that is stricter on `exports`. Happy to send a PR.

Optional bonus: a static linter that walks `packages/*/package.json`, finds any `bin`-listed file doing `import 'foo/sub/path.js'`, and verifies `foo`'s `exports` map permits that subpath. Would catch this entire class of bug pre-publish.

## Workaround for users hitting this today

```bash
# Use the develop branch CLI directly — confirmed working:
git clone https://github.com/elizaos/eliza.git && cd eliza
bun install
bun run --cwd packages/elizaos build
node packages/elizaos/dist/cli.js create my-app --template project --yes

# Or pin a pre-release tag:
npm i -g elizaos@beta
```9:Ta3d,## 🤖 Runtime Open Federation × ElizaOS

Hey ElizaOS community! 👋

**Runtime Open Federation** is a permissionless, open AI agent network now live. Any Eliza agent can register, pick up tasks from the shared task market, and earn **USDC on Base chain**.

### Why ElizaOS + Federation = 💥

- Eliza’s plugin architecture maps perfectly to federation capabilities
- **No API key** to join — open network
- **MCP 1.0 + AgentProtocol** compatible
- **USDC rewards** via Base chain (25% pool for contributors)
- Cross-framework: your Eliza agent works alongside CrewAI, LangChain, AutoGPT agents

### Integration snippet (TypeScript)

```typescript
// Register your Eliza agent in the federation
const res = await fetch(
  'https://kjtirbnxxymeumycrhqv.supabase.co/functions/v1/runtime-public-federation/register',
  {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
    body: JSON.stringify({
      agent_id: 'my-eliza-agent',
      capabilities: ['conversation', 'social', 'autonomous'],
      framework: 'elizaos',
      endpoint: 'https://your-eliza-instance.com'
    })
  }
);

// Get open tasks
const manifest = await fetch(
   'https://kjtirbnxxymeumycrhqv.supabase.co/functions/v1/runtime-public-federation/manifest'
).then(r => r.json());
```

🔗 **Federation Manifest**: https://kjtirbnxxymeumycrhqv.supabase.co/functions/v1/runtime-public-federation/manifest

I have successfully implemented both the voice control system for the Interactive Canvas and the default dashboard interface toggle preference in the Eliza Cloud platform.

---

## 1. Default UI Interface & Layout Preference

### Core Implementation
- **State & Persistence:** Added `defaultUiMode` (`'canvas' | 'classic'`) state and the action `setDefaultUiMode(mode)` in the Zustand store [canvas-store.ts](file:///Users/home/Documents/eliza/packages/cloud-frontend/src/lib/stores/canvas-store.ts). This preference is persisted in the local storage config.
- **Settings tab:** Added the **Default interface** configuration card inside the general settings page [general-tab.tsx](file:///Users/home/Documents/eliza/packages/cloud-frontend/src/dashboard/settings/_components/tabs/general-tab.tsx). Users can choose their startup layout using a drop-down selector.
- **Layout switcher:** Integrated the conditional layout render inside the layout router [App.tsx](file:///Users/home/Documents/eliza/packages/cloud-frontend/src/App.tsx).
  - If `canvasOpen` is true, `<CanvasLayout />` renders.
  - If `canvasOpen` is false, `<DashboardLayout />` renders instead, unmounting the canvas to prevent page navigation from triggering unintended canvas events.
  - While in `<DashboardLayout />`, the floating assistant pill [cloud-assistant-pill.tsx](file:///Users/home/Documents/eliza/packages/cloud-frontend/src/components/canvas/cloud-assistant-pill.tsx) remains overlayed in the bottom-center of the viewport, so pressing `cmd + k` or clicking the orb seamlessly slides back into the Interactive Canvas.

---

## 2. Voice-Driven Canvas Interaction UX

### Core Features
- **Unified Voice Hooks:** Integrated `useVoiceChat` and `useContinuousChat` from `@elizaos/ui/hooks` with a stabilized React Ref (`handleSendRef`) to avoid stale closure state during voice/speech callbacks.
- **Dual-Mode UX State Flow:**
  - **Text Mode:** Canvas is open with the text input bar expanded. A microphone button is available next to the close button to switch to voice mode.
  - **Voice Mode:** Input bar collapses into a floating circular orb (56x56 px). centring states like recording (`Mic`), thinking (`Loader2`), or speaking (`Volume2` bounce animation). Real-time preview transcript is shown in a small card overlay.
- **TTS Responses:** Automatically speaks out assistant responses on query execution success, along with helpful voice-guided prompts.

d:Tcdf,## Eliza Cloud Apps (Product 2) — per-tenant-isolated container hosting

Lets users deploy arbitrary container apps with a **per-tenant database, sign-in, a per-app URL, and metered hosting billing**, isolated from the shared Eliza **agent** system. Layered onto 2AM's `/api/v1/containers` surface — never rebuilt.

**Draft for review (nubs + Shaw). Not requesting merge yet.** All changes are additive and the agent plane is byte-for-byte untouched; the apps path is feature-gated off until its env/infra is configured.

### What's here (all in `packages/cloud-shared`, additive)
- **Per-tenant DB isolation** — `tenant_db_clusters` pool (race-safe `tryClaimSlot`), `SqlTenantDbProvisioner` + `DirectPgExecutor`, composer `makeTenantDbProvisioning`. Strategy: DATABASE+ROLE-per-tenant with `REVOKE CONNECT … FROM PUBLIC` (a hard auth-layer boundary), pluggable provider. Migration `0140` (journal-registered).
- **Per-tenant network isolation** — `--internal` network + `--cap-drop=ALL`/`no-new-privileges`/`pids-limit`, default-deny egress proxy. Never `NET_ADMIN`/tun (those stay agent-only).
- **Repo→image build pipeline** — pure ref/build-cmd builders + `AppImageBuilder` (over an injected SSH/exec seam).
- **Deploy orchestration + adapters** — `AppDeployRunner` (ensure tenant DB → create container row carrying the per-tenant DSN → enqueue `CONTAINER_PROVISION` → link via `apps.metadata.containerId`), `AppContainerStore` (maps host placement into `containers.metadata`), `ContainerJobsWriter`.
- **Per-app URL** — reuses the shared `derivePublicHostname` (ingress source of truth) to stamp `public_hostname`/`load_balancer_url` + `apps.production_url`.
- **`APP_DEPLOY` trigger** — the Worker enqueues `APP_DEPLOY` (pg-free); the daemon claims it and runs the node runner, so `pg`/SSH never touch the workerd request path.

### Verified for real (not mocks)
- **Real Postgres 16:** cross-tenant connection rejected — `permission denied for database` (`REVOKE CONNECT`).
- **Real Docker:** `scripts/verify-e2e-deploy.sh` 5/5 — build user image → provision per-tenant DB → run `--internal` isolated container with its DSN → reaches its own DB + serves its URL → rejected from other tenants' DBs → no egress.
- **Real drizzle schema (PGlite):** `scripts/verify-deploy-db-writes.sh` 10/10 — adapter inserts, the per-tenant-DSN-in-`environment_vars` invariant, URL stamping.
- **236 unit tests green, typecheck + biome clean.** Migration `0140` applies in the full real chain.

### Remaining rollout (NOT in this PR — needs infra + review)
1. Two boot one-liners during apps-infra rollout: cloud-api `configureAppsDeployTrigger()` + daemon `configureAppsDeployBackend({ registry, buildExec })`.
2. Apps **data-plane infra** via terraform+CI: app docker node(s), the tenant Postgres cluster, image registry, egress proxy.
3. VPS scratch kernel-validation (read-only / non-destructive).

### Non-collision
Rebased-clean on `develop` (new files + appended enum keys / switch cases). Does not touch the agent path or 2AM's hot files (`v1/containers/route.ts`, `[id]/route.ts`, `cron/container-billing/route.ts`, `db/schemas/containers.ts`, `hetzner-client/client.ts`). Billing-field init (`next_billing_at`) is a coordination item with 2AM's #8273.
e:T9b9,## Summary

Adds Electric Sync (PGlite ↔ Electric Cloud) and a write-back service for bidirectional data synchronization.

### What changed

- **Electric Sync** — `PGliteClientManager` now registers `electricSync()` extension when `ELIZA_ELECTRIC_SYNC_URL` is set. Per-agent WHERE filtering (`agent_id = ${agentId}`) with literal SQL values (required by Electric Cloud v1.6.x). UUID validation prevents SQL injection.
- **Write-Back Service** — New `WriteBackService` forwards local PGlite writes to the cloud API. `PgliteDatabaseAdapter` notifies the service after inserts/upserts/deletes on synced tables.
- **Caddy Reverse Proxy** — `plugins/plugin-sql/caddy/electric-proxy.Caddyfile` forwards all requests to `api.electric-sql.cloud` with auth query params injected from env vars.
- **Tests** — e2e write-back test against Electric Cloud (env-var gated, skips gracefully). Deferred sync startup tests. Live query tests. 14 write-back unit tests.
- **Docs** — README updated with Electric Sync section and expanded env var table. `.env.example` created documenting all Electric Cloud and sync env vars.
- **Config** — `.gitignore` tracks `.env.example`. Docker Compose for local Electric+Postgres test stack.

### How to test

```bash
# Start Caddy proxy (one-time)
export ELECTRIC_CLOUD_SOURCE_ID=...
export ELECTRIC_CLOUD_SECRET=...
caddy run --config plugins/plugin-sql/caddy/electric-proxy.Caddyfile &

# Run e2e test
cd plugins/plugin-sql/src
ELECTRIC_CLOUD_SOURCE_ID=... ELECTRIC_CLOUD_SECRET=... npx vitest run __tests__/integration/electric-write-back.test.ts
```

### Checks
- ✅ Typecheck clean
- ✅ 47/47 tests pass (8 test files)
- ✅ E2e test exits code 0 against Electric Cloud

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->10:Tb27,## What

Closes the production gap that left the capped self-spend autonomy mechanism (merged in #8250) unreachable by a live agent. A `/economics` sub-agent can now autonomously drive the monetized-app loop through the parent-agent Cloud command broker.

## Why

`runParentAgentBroker` / `runCloudCommand` (the only emitter of `spend_auto_authorized`) had **no production caller**: nothing parsed a child's `USE_SKILL parent-agent {…}` request, `buildSkillsManifest({ virtualSkills })` was never called, and the autonomy was only proven via Vitest. See `plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/docs/economics-goal-runbook.md` (bundled/updated here).

## How (the three gaps, closed)

1. **Dispatch** — new `parent-agent-dispatch.ts`: a string/escape-aware extractor for `USE_SKILL parent-agent {json}` (handles streaming/partial JSON, braces inside strings, nested params, markdown backticks, malformed markers) + a dispatch helper that runs the directive through `runParentAgentBroker` and streams `result.text` back via `acp.sendToSession`. Wired into `SubAgentRouter.handleEvent`: accumulates the child's `message` chunks, dispatches the first complete directive, caps round-trips via `ACPX_SUB_AGENT_ROUND_TRIP_CAP`. **Marker-guarded** — only acts on text containing `USE_SKILL parent-agent`, which ordinary coding tasks never emit, so the session-event hot path is inert for every other flow.
2. **Advertise** — `spawnAgentForTask` writes a `SKILLS.md` for `capabilityProfile === "economics"` tasks via `buildSkillsManifest(..., { recommendedSlugs: ["build-monetized-app","eliza-cloud"], virtualSkills: [PARENT_AGENT_BROKER_MANIFEST_ENTRY] })` so the child learns the slug + arg contract. Best-effort (never fatal).
3. **Estimate** — the broker's unknown-cost stall now returns an *actionable* instruction (fetch a quote with `domains.check`, retry with `params.spendEstimateUsd`) instead of a human-only yes/no, and the manifest guidance advertises the same pattern — so `domains.buy` self-authorizes within the cap without a human turn.

## Verification

- New `parent-agent-dispatch.test.ts` (11 cases): extractor edge cases + the broker→`sendToSession` bridge end to end via the network-free `list-cloud-commands` mode.
- Existing `parent-agent-broker.test.ts` + `skill-manifest.test.ts` still green (no regression from the preview/guidance change).
- Plugin typecheck clean; biome clean.
- End-to-end (a real `spend_auto_authorized` on `domains.buy`/`containers.create`) still needs a live ACP backend — the runbook documents the exact steps.

## Safety

The hot-path hook is a no-op unless the child emits the literal `USE_SKILL parent-agent` marker. Paid/mutating commands remain gated by the existing capped spend allowance (`ELIZA_AGENT_SPEND_CAP_USD`, default 0 = confirm everything); destructive commands always require a human.11:T27e1,## Why

The tenant Postgres + private network are physically a single shared piece of
infra across staging + production app workers, but they were being modelled
per-env inside `apps-data-plane/`. That meant a staging vs prod apply could in
theory rebuild the tenant DB out from under the other env, and the two state
files each thought they owned the same Hetzner-side network. Splitting the
module pins the truth in TF.

## What changed

### New `apps-shared/` module — single shared backend

Owns the resources that are physically one thing:

- `hcloud_network.apps` + `hcloud_network_subnet.apps` (`10.30.0.0/16` → `10.30.1.0/24`)
- `hcloud_firewall.tenant_db` (port 22 from operator CIDRs)
- `hcloud_volume.tenant_db_data` (200 GB default)
- `hcloud_server.tenant_db` (`cpx42` default, **named `eliza-app-tenant`** — no env suffix because the resource is shared)
- `hcloud_server_network.tenant_db` (`10.30.1.10` private IP, stable)
- `hcloud_volume_attachment.tenant_db_data`
- `random_password.tenant_db_admin`
- `cloud-init/tenant-db.yaml.tftpl` (copied verbatim from apps-data-plane — latest fixed version with the bootcmd + systemd-oneshot ordering)
- `versions.tf` / `providers.tf` / `variables.tf` / `outputs.tf`
- `backend.hcl` (single shared backend file → `hetzner/apps-shared/shared.tfstate`)
- `tfvars/shared.tfvars.example`

Outputs: `apps_network_id`, `apps_subnet_id`, `apps_subnet_cidr`, `tenant_db_private_ip` (10.30.1.10), `tenant_db_public_ip`, `tenant_db_admin_dsn` (sensitive).

Note: I didn't move the `app_node` firewall into shared — it's per-env (its name carries the env suffix and the operator CIDR list could legitimately diverge between staging and prod), so the split stays clean: shared = the actual shared infra, per-env = everything env-scoped.

### Updated `apps-data-plane/` — slimmer, per-env

- Drops every tenant_db resource + the network/subnet.
- Adds `data "terraform_remote_state" "apps_shared"` pointing at the shared R2 state file.
- `hcloud_server_network.app_node` attaches to `data.terraform_remote_state.apps_shared.outputs.apps_network_id`.
- `cloud-init/app-node.yaml.tftpl` keeps its `tenant_db_host` template var, now sourced from the remote state output.
- Outputs no longer include any `tenant_db_*` field.
- Dropped the `random` provider (no longer used) so `.terraform.lock.hcl` shrinks.

### Workflows

- **NEW** `.github/workflows/terraform-apps-shared.yml` — `pull_request` (fmt + validate) + `workflow_dispatch` with `action` (plan/apply) and optional `replace` input. **No environment input** — module owns one set of resources. Uses repo-level vars `APPS_SHARED_OPERATOR_SSH_KEY` + `APPS_SHARED_OPERATOR_INGRESS_CIDRS`.
- **UPDATED** `.github/workflows/terraform-apps-data-plane.yml` — same per-env dispatch shape; header explains tenant_db lives elsewhere now; `replace` notice mentions `hcloud_server.tenant_db` is a no-op here (use the apps-shared workflow). R2 state creds still cover both this module's state AND the apps-shared remote-state read.

### Misc

- ARCHITECTURE.md updated: tree shows `eliza-app-tenant` shared once, the per-env list only carries app_node servers.
- `apps-data-plane/README.md` rewritten around the split + the apply order.

## State migration plan — operator runs ONCE, manually

There's existing state in `hetzner/apps-data-plane/staging.tfstate` (the tenant DB was deployed on staging before this refactor). Move it to the new shared backend before any new apply.

Order of operations:

1. **Pause both workflows on develop** so nobody apply-races the move:
   ```
   gh workflow disable terraform-apps-data-plane.yml
   gh workflow disable terraform-apps-shared.yml
   ```

2. **Pull both state files locally**:
   ```bash
   export AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID=...    # R2_STATE_ACCESS_KEY_ID
   export AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY=...
   export HCLOUD_TOKEN=...         # HCLOUD_APPS_TOKEN

   # source: staging apps-data-plane (pre-refactor layout)
   cd packages/cloud-infra/cloud/terraform/hetzner/apps-data-plane
   git checkout develop  # the OLD code path, with tenant_db in this module
   terraform init -backend-config=backend-staging.hcl
   terraform state pull > /tmp/staging-old.tfstate

   # destination: brand-new apps-shared backend (empty)
   cd ../apps-shared
   git checkout refactor/apps-shared-module
   terraform init -backend-config=backend.hcl
   ```

3. **Move the tenant DB + deps from the old staging state into the new shared state**. Run these from the `apps-shared/` working directory (`-state` reads source from disk, `-state-out` writes destination):
   ```bash
   terraform state mv -state=/tmp/staging-old.tfstate -state-out=/tmp/shared-new.tfstate \
     hcloud_network.apps                   hcloud_network.apps
   terraform state mv -state=/tmp/staging-old.tfstate -state-out=/tmp/shared-new.tfstate \
     hcloud_network_subnet.apps            hcloud_network_subnet.apps
   terraform state mv -state=/tmp/staging-old.tfstate -state-out=/tmp/shared-new.tfstate \
     hcloud_firewall.tenant_db             hcloud_firewall.tenant_db
   terraform state mv -state=/tmp/staging-old.tfstate -state-out=/tmp/shared-new.tfstate \
     hcloud_volume.tenant_db_data          hcloud_volume.tenant_db_data
   terraform state mv -state=/tmp/staging-old.tfstate -state-out=/tmp/shared-new.tfstate \
     hcloud_server.tenant_db               hcloud_server.tenant_db
   terraform state mv -state=/tmp/staging-old.tfstate -state-out=/tmp/shared-new.tfstate \
     hcloud_server_network.tenant_db       hcloud_server_network.tenant_db
   terraform state mv -state=/tmp/staging-old.tfstate -state-out=/tmp/shared-new.tfstate \
     hcloud_volume_attachment.tenant_db_data hcloud_volume_attachment.tenant_db_data
   terraform state mv -state=/tmp/staging-old.tfstate -state-out=/tmp/shared-new.tfstate \
     random_password.tenant_db_admin       random_password.tenant_db_admin
   ```

4. **Push both updated state files**:
   ```bash
   # write the new shared state
   cd packages/cloud-infra/cloud/terraform/hetzner/apps-shared
   terraform state push /tmp/shared-new.tfstate

   # write the slimmed-down staging state back
   cd ../apps-data-plane
   terraform state push /tmp/staging-old.tfstate
   ```

5. **Verify both modules plan against reality**:
   ```bash
   cd ../apps-shared && terraform plan -var-file=shared.tfvars
   # EXPECTED (apply this — these are the intended renames + label updates):
   #   ~ hcloud_network.apps        — labels: tier "apps-data-plane" -> "apps-shared",
   #                                  drop environment key (NAME rename suppressed via
   #                                  lifecycle.ignore_changes=[name])
   #   ~ hcloud_volume.tenant_db_data   — same labels diff; name rename suppressed
   #   ~ hcloud_firewall.tenant_db      — same labels diff; name rename suppressed
   #   ~ hcloud_server.tenant_db        — same labels diff; name + user_data +
   #                                       image + ssh_keys all suppressed
   # All four diffs are IN-PLACE (Hetzner PATCH on labels), no destroy, volume +
   # data + private IP all stable. Run `terraform apply` once to converge.
   #
   # NOT EXPECTED (abort + rollback if you see any of these):
   #   - hcloud_server.tenant_db destroyed / replaced
   #   - hcloud_volume.tenant_db_data destroyed / replaced
   #   - any "+ -> destroy" line

   cd ../apps-data-plane && terraform plan -var-file=staging.tfvars
   # expected: no changes — app_node + firewall + DNS already in this state,
   # the new data source just reads the (now shared) network id.
   ```

6. **Re-enable workflows**:
   ```
   gh workflow enable terraform-apps-data-plane.yml
   gh workflow enable terraform-apps-shared.yml
   ```

7. **Done.** Future applies of `apps-data-plane` for staging and production both consume the same shared state.

### About the Hetzner-side name (`eliza-apps-tenantdb-staging` → `eliza-app-tenant`)

The existing tenant DB Hetzner server is named `eliza-apps-tenantdb-staging`. After the state move, TF wants the resource called `eliza-app-tenant` — but `hcloud_server.tenant_db.lifecycle.ignore_changes` already includes `name`, so **TF will NOT rename the server Hetzner-side**. The operator can rename it via the Hetzner Console (cosmetic only — labels, IPs, volumes are all stable).

## Rollback plan

If anything goes wrong after the state move but before reality changes:

1. Revert this PR (`git revert` on develop).
2. Restore the staging state from the R2 versioned bucket (R2 keeps prior object versions — the pre-move tfstate is still there as the previous version).
3. Or push `/tmp/staging-old.tfstate.bak` back (`terraform state push -force` from the backup we kept locally before step 4).

The tenant DB itself is never destroyed during this migration — `terraform state mv` only edits the state graph, no apply runs against the cloud. Worst case after a botched revert is "state thinks the tenant DB is in one module, reality has it; next apply tries to recreate it" → operator rolls back state instead, no DB loss.

## Test plan

- [x] `terraform fmt -check -recursive` clean in both modules
- [x] `terraform init -backend=false && terraform validate` succeeds in both modules
- [x] Both workflows parse as valid YAML
- [ ] PR CI: validate job passes for both `terraform-apps-shared.yml` and `terraform-apps-data-plane.yml`
- [ ] Operator runs the state migration plan above on staging
- [ ] `apps-shared` `plan` returns no changes after the state move
- [ ] `apps-data-plane` staging `plan` returns no changes after the state move
- [ ] Then `apps-data-plane` production `plan` (first apply against this module for prod) shows only the **new** app_node + firewall + DNS for production, no tenant_db touch

## Notes

- No flag toggles — `apps-data-plane` no longer has a `provision_tenant_db` knob, the split is hard.
- No legacy fallback path — the old per-env tenant_db code is gone from `apps-data-plane/`. The state move is the only path forward.
- `app_node` firewall stayed in `apps-data-plane` (per-env), only the tenant_db firewall moved to shared. If we ever want the app_node firewall shared too it's a separate refactor.
12:Tacb,> **Draft / proposal.** Slice 1 of an "Eliza Cloud Apps" direction (turnkey building blocks for third-party/hosted apps). Self-contained, new sub-export only — **touches no server, data-plane, or agent code.**

## What

New `@elizaos/cloud-sdk/app-auth` sub-export: a small, **dependency-free** client that lets any third-party app authenticate its users through Eliza Cloud — "Sign in with Eliza Cloud." It wraps the **already-live** app-auth flow; no backend changes.

## The flow it wraps (built against the real contract)

No PKCE — the consent step is gated by the user's existing Eliza Cloud session, and `redirect_uri` is validated against the app's registered allowed origins (`/api/v1/apps/:id/public`).

1. **Authorize** — `buildAppAuthorizeUrl({ appId, redirectUri, state })` → `https://www.elizacloud.ai/app-auth/authorize?app_id=…&redirect_uri=…&state=…` (matches what the hosted `<AuthorizeContent>` consent page reads).
2. **Callback** — Eliza redirects back: `<redirectUri>?code=eac_…&state=…` on success, or `?error=access_denied&error_description=…&state=…` on denial (matches `buildAppAuthorizeCompletionRedirect`).
3. **Exchange** — `exchangeAppAuthorizeCode(code)` → `GET /api/v1/app-auth/session` with `Authorization: Bearer eac_…` → `{ user: { id, email, name, avatar, createdAt }, app }`. The `eac_` code is opaque, single-use, 5-min TTL (matches `app-auth-codes.ts` + the `session` route).

## API

```ts
import { ElizaCloudAppAuth } from "@elizaos/cloud-sdk/app-auth";
const auth = new ElizaCloudAppAuth({ appId, redirectUri });
location.href = auth.authorizeUrl(state);                  // 1
const { user, app } = await auth.completeSignIn(req.url);  // 2 + 3 (server-side)
```

Also exports the standalone `buildAppAuthorizeUrl`, `parseAppAuthorizeCallback`, `exchangeAppAuthorizeCode`, `looksLikeAppAuthCode`, `ElizaCloudAuthError`, and the `APP_AUTH_*` constants. All accept optional `fetchImpl` / `baseUrl` / `apiBaseUrl` for tests + custom origins. Defaults reuse the package's `DEFAULT_ELIZA_CLOUD_BASE_URL` / `DEFAULT_ELIZA_CLOUD_API_BASE_URL`.

## Tests

15 unit tests (mocked fetch): URL build (+ custom base, optional state, validation), callback parsing across input shapes (URL string / `URLSearchParams` / bare query / `Location`), code exchange with Bearer + `X-App-Id` assertions, custom `apiBaseUrl`, and error paths (non-`eac_` short-circuit, `401` → `ElizaCloudAuthError` with server code). biome + tsgo clean. Pure TS, zero new deps.

## Why draft

It's a proposal for the Apps direction — happy to adjust the surface, the home (sub-export vs root barrel), or naming. Follows the existing `./cloud-setup-session` sub-export pattern. Documented in the package CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md.13:T870,## What

16:Tcb0,## Summary

The advanced-memory `summaryEvaluator` (`packages/core/src/features/advanced-memory/evaluators/memory-items.ts`) has two coupled bugs that surface in busy rooms — the second was found while reviewing the fix for the first.

### Bug 1 — unbounded first-summary prompt → permanent `context_length_exceeded` loop
`prepareSummary()` caps the work window at `summaryMaxNewMessages` (default 50 / `MemoryService` config 20) and advances `lastMessageOffset` by that bounded `summarizationMessages` slice. But `prompt()` rendered the full `allDialogueMessages` (fetched with `limit: 1000`, unbounded by tokens) on the **no-existing-summary** branch:

```ts
const recentMessages = prepared.existingSummary
  ? prepared.summarizationMessages   // bounded (≤ maxNewMessages)
  : prepared.allDialogueMessages;    // up to 1000 messages, unbounded by tokens
```

In a high-traffic room the first summary therefore sends a request far over the model's context window (observed live: a ~3.1M-token request to a 131k-limit endpoint → `400 context_length_exceeded`). Because the request fails, the summary never stores, so `existingSummary` stays null and the **same oversized request retries forever**. It also double-counts messages on the next run (the offset only advanced by the bounded slice).

### Bug 2 — first store jumps the offset past unsummarized messages (silent data loss)
On the first store (no existing summary), the offset was set to the whole backlog while only the bounded slice was summarized:

```ts
messageCount: prepared.totalDialogueCount,      // e.g. 1000
lastMessageOffset: prepared.totalDialogueCount, // e.g. 1000
```

So after summarizing the first ≤20 messages, the offset jumps to 1000 and messages 20→1000 are **never summarized** on subsequent runs. (The existing-summary branch already does the right thing via `newOffset = lastOffset + summarizationMessages.length`.)

## Fix
- `prompt()` always uses the bounded `summarizationMessages` slice.
- The first-store branch advances `lastMessageOffset`/`messageCount` by the summarized slice (`newOffset`), mirroring the existing-summary branch, so the rolling summary builds up **incrementally and bounded** instead of crashing or skipping.
- Drop the now-unused `allDialogueMessages` field from `SummaryPrepared` (the local var stays — it still feeds `totalDialogueCount` and the slice). No external consumer reads the field.
- Add regression tests: the prompt is bounded to `summarizationMessages` regardless of `existingSummary`, and the first store advances the offset by the slice (not the backlog).

## Notes
- Commit 1 is a **pure tabs→spaces biome reformat** of the file (no logic change — `git diff -w` is empty) so the logic change in commit 2 is a minimal, reviewable ~14-line diff. The file was tab-indented; `biome.json` enforces 2-space.
- A pre-existing follow-up (left out of scope): the cap is by message **count**, not tokens, so pathologically large individual messages could still exceed context within the bounded slice — a token-budget cap would be a separate enhancement.

## Test
```
bun run --cwd packages/core test -- --run memory-items   # 3 passed
bun run --cwd packages/core typecheck                     # clean
```
17:Tb1e,## What

Fixes CRITICAL leak #2 from #8342: **a deleted app with an ISOLATED per-tenant DB never DROPped its Postgres or released its cluster slot** — so we kept paying for a stranded DB and permanently burned 1/`max_databases` of a cluster's finite slots.

## Why it was leaking

The deprovision logic already exists end to end — `deprovisionTenantDbForApp` + `cleanupDatabase`'s isolated branch + `releaseSlot` (`GREATEST(0, database_count - 1)`). It was **runtime-dead**: its only caller is the cloud-api **Worker**, whose `UserDatabaseService` singleton has no provisioning backend — correctly, because the `DROP` needs `pg`, which can't load on workerd. So the isolated branch silently no-opped, and on `appsRepository.delete` the `app_databases` row cascaded away, losing the only handle to the DB.

## Fix — mirror the `APP_DEPLOY` split

Run the DROP where `pg` lives (the daemon), triggered from the Worker:

- **New `APP_DB_DEPROVISION` job type** + `app-db-deprovision-job-service.ts` (same shape as `app-deploy-job-service.ts`). The Worker enqueues it (pg-free), carrying the app's **encrypted** DSN in the payload — it's already ciphertext at rest in `app_databases`, so copying it adds no exposure, and it **survives the app row's cascade-delete** so the daemon can still resolve the cluster.
- **`executeJob`** gains one additive `case` → `dispatchAppDbDeprovisionJob` (decrypts node-side, delegates to the injected deprovisioner: DROP DB + ROLE, then `releaseSlot`).
- **`apps-deploy-backend`** arms the deprovisioner with the same `makeTenantDbProvisioning()` it already builds (both env-DSN and encrypted modes).
- **`bootstrap-app`** wires the Worker enqueuer under the existing `APPS_DEPLOY_ENABLED` gate.
- **`apps.delete`** passes the owning org/user so the job row can be formed.

## Scope / safety

- Targets the **encrypted (canonical Product-2) path**. The env-DSN staging shortcut stores the DSN on the container rather than `app_databases`, so its teardown is a separate small follow-up (noted; staging isn't serving yet).
- **Idempotent + fail-soft:** an enqueue or DROP hiccup logs and lets the delete proceed; the missing-host / unknown-cluster cases return `{ deprovisioned: false }` rather than throwing. An orphan-DB reconciler remains the backstop.
- Purely additive to the agent/container dispatch paths — no change to `eliza-sandbox`, `container-billing`, or `hetzner-client`.

## Tests

10 new (all green), plus `typecheck` + `biome` clean:
- `app-db-deprovision-job-service.test.ts` — payload validation, dispatch decrypt+delegate, unwired-throw, enqueue shape.
- `user-database-deprovision.test.ts` — the `cleanupDatabase` enqueue seam: fires only with an isolated URI + wired enqueuer + known org; no-ops otherwise.

18:T7c7,Closes the **isolated-DB leak** half of #8342.

## Problem
Deleting an app with its own per-tenant Postgres **never DROPped** the DATABASE/ROLE and **never released** the cluster slot — `SqlTenantDbProvisioner.deprovision` had zero callers, there was no `releaseSlot` (only `tryClaimSlot` incremented), and `cleanupDatabase` only handled the legacy Neon path. So every deleted isolated app stranded a live DB we keep paying for **and** burned one of the cluster's 2000 finite slots permanently.

## Fix
- **`releaseSlot(clusterId)`** — the counterpart to `tryClaimSlot`; `GREATEST(0, count-1)` so a double-release can't drive the count negative. Plus **`findByHost(host)`** to resolve the owning cluster from the host in the app's stored DSN.
- **`deprovisionTenantDbForApp`** (pure, DI'd) — parse the DSN host → resolve the cluster → DROP the DB+role → **then** release the slot (order matters: a failed DROP leaves the slot counted, so we never free a slot for a still-live DB). Fail-safe no-ops for a shared/unknown DSN.
- **`SqlTenantDbProvisioning.deprovisionForApp`** wires the use-case over the repo + admin executor; `make-tenant-db-provisioning` injects the real backends.
- **`UserDatabaseService.cleanupDatabase`** now tears down the isolated path too (was Neon-only), gated on the provisioning backend being wired (the daemon — `DROP` needs `pg`); Worker-side it's a no-op.

## Tests
DSN-host parsing, drop-then-release ordering, fail-safe no-ops (no host / unknown cluster), a failed-DROP-keeps-the-slot case, the composer path, and the not-configured guard. **31 pass / 0 fail**; typecheck + biome clean.

## Scope / remaining
Additive, our tenant-DB lane only (no agent path, no 2AM billing files). The deprovision fires when `cleanupDatabase` runs on the **daemon**; wiring app-delete to trigger a daemon-side cleanup (an enqueue) is the final integration step — gated on the live daemon, like the rest of the apps lane. Pairs with #8338 (opt-in isolated DB).19:T937,## The gap (found by testing the full deploy on staging)
App containers run on a per-app `--internal` Docker network with **zero egress**
(proven — a container on it can't even reach `8.8.8.8`). But that *also* blocks the
app's **own tenant Postgres**, which lives on a different host/node. So a deploy
would start the container, yet the app could **never connect to its database**.

The earlier 5/5 isolation proof missed this because it tested app↔DB and the
egress-block on **separate** networks — never combined. The existing `egressProxyUrl`
mechanism only covers **HTTP**; a Postgres connection is raw TCP.

## Fix — the per-app DB "ambassador" (standard ambassador/sidecar pattern)
A tiny **trusted `socat` forwarder** is attached to the app's `--internal` network and
forwards `:5432` to **only** that tenant DB. The app's `DATABASE_URL` host is rewritten
to the ambassador. So:
- the untrusted app keeps **zero general egress** (the proven `--internal` posture is unchanged);
- the single-purpose forwarder is the **only** thing that reaches the DB, and **only** the DB (fixed socat target);
- `REVOKE CONNECT` still isolates the actual databases, so a shared cluster stays per-tenant safe.

```
[app :3000]──app-net (--internal, no egress)──[socat ambassador]──bridge──▶ tenant DB :5432
     └ DATABASE_URL=…@app-db-<slug>:5432/…            (forwards ONLY to the tenant DB)
```

## Changes
- **`app-db-ambassador.ts`** (new): pure builders — `ambassadorName`, `parseDsnEndpoint`,
  `rewriteDsnToAmbassador`, `buildEnsureAmbassadorCmds`, teardown.
- **`app-container-provider.ts`**: `provision()` stands up the ambassador + rewrites the DSN
  when a `DATABASE_URL` is present; `delete()` tears it down.
- **`container-executor-deps.ts`**: `APPS_DB_EGRESS_NETWORK` / `APPS_DB_AMBASSADOR_IMAGE` env.

## Verification
- **13/13** new unit tests; biome + typecheck clean.
- **Real-infra capstone on staging — 5/5:** app network `--internal`, DSN rewritten,
  **isolated app reaches its tenant DB *through the ambassador***, internet egress **still
  blocked** (8.8.8.8), socat target pinned to the DB. (Throwaway resources, cleaned up.)

(socat is transparent TCP); the staging tenant PG has no TLS so the proof localized `sslmode`.1a:T972,## Why

Co-authored-by: wakesync <shadow@shad0w.xyz>1b:Tb45,## Why

Today we hit `"Agent creation requires owner or admin session"` (403) when the provisioning daemon tried to register a new Dedicated agent with Steward. Same shape as the 502 we just fixed on `/api/auth/steward-refresh` — Steward's authorization-signature middleware was rejecting daemon-signed requests, so it fell through to the session-owner check and 403'd.

Root cause: the daemon had its own copy of `buildStewardCanonicalRequest` / `buildStewardSignedHeaders` in `docker-sandbox-provider.ts` that diverged from the working `cloud-api/src/steward/sign.ts` helper. The daemon copy included `X-Steward-Request-Timestamp` in the canonical hash; upstream Steward (and `cloud-api/src/steward/embedded.ts` proxy mirror) hash the slot as the empty string. Daemon HMACs verified as invalid → fell through → 403.

The two best-effort cleanup `curl -X DELETE` calls also went out unsigned, so every failed registration left a Steward ghost record. That's the trail behind the `deletion_pending` agents users sometimes saw on staging.

## What

- Move `signStewardMutatingRequest` + `buildStewardCanonicalRequest` from `packages/cloud-api/src/steward/sign.ts` to `packages/cloud-shared/src/lib/steward/sign.ts` so the daemon (which lives in cloud-shared) can use the same helper as the Worker bypass routes.
- Replace the local `buildStewardCanonicalRequest` / `buildStewardSignedHeaders` in `docker-sandbox-provider.ts` with a thin wrapper that calls the shared helper and returns a flat `Record<string, string>` (the Python shim and curl path need a plain object, not a `Headers`).
- Add `buildSignedDeleteAgentCurl` for the two cleanup paths (failed container create, missing Headscale registration). Same signing as register; passes `-H` flags built from the signed header set.
- Re-point Worker imports (`auth/steward-refresh`, `auth/steward-nonce-exchange`, `__tests__/steward-sign.test.ts`) at the new shared location.

Behavior change: signed requests now match upstream Steward's canonical-request byte-for-byte. Unsigned cleanup DELETEs are now signed when `STEWARD_REQUEST_SIGNING_SECRET` is configured. No new env vars; no schema changes.

## Test plan

- [x] Unit: `bun test packages/cloud-api/__tests__/steward-sign.test.ts` → 4 pass / 0 fail
- [x] Typecheck: `bunx tsgo --noEmit` clean on cloud-shared + cloud-api for touched files
- [ ] CI green
- [ ] Manual: after merge + daemon redeploy on staging admin (`46.224.1.246`), retry "New Agent → Dedicated → develop image" from staging.elizacloud.ai — should reach `status=running` instead of failing with 403 on Steward registration
- [ ] Manual: agent deletion goes through (no `deletion_pending` ghost)

## Follow-up (not in this PR)

- `STEWARD_REQUEST_SIGNING_KEY_ID` env var declaration in `cloud-worker-env.ts:68` and `wrangler.toml:171` is now orphan — no longer consumed. Worth a cleanup pass.1c:T8c3,## What
The deploy orchestrator provisioned an isolated per-tenant Postgres for **every** app and injected `DATABASE_URL`/`POSTGRES_URL` unconditionally. That forces a database (and a tenant-DB cluster) on apps that don't need one — e.g. a stateless SPA + stateless API like elocute.

Make the database a **per-app choice**, addable later, with no migration:

- **`apps.metadata.databaseMode`** (jsonb): `"none"` (default) or `"isolated"`.
  - **none** → stateless: no DB provisioned, **no** `DATABASE_URL`.
  - **isolated** → the app's OWN per-tenant Postgres, injected under both `DATABASE_URL` + `POSTGRES_URL`.
- `deployApp` reads the mode and only calls `ensureTenantDb` + injects the DSN when `isolated`. `DefaultAppDeployRunner` resolves the mode from the app.
- **Add a DB later — seamless + lossless:** `PUT /api/v1/apps/:id/database {mode:"isolated"}` then redeploy. `ensureTenantDb` is create-if-not-exists, so the DB materializes and wires in. Flipping back to `none` stops injecting the DSN but **leaves the database intact** (destroying tenant data is a separate explicit action). `GET` returns the current mode + binding.

The endpoint is **Worker-safe** (only writes the metadata flag; provisioning happens on the next deploy on the daemon). The load-bearing invariant is preserved: an isolated app gets its **own** DSN, never the shared agent URL, never a silent PGlite fallback.

## Why
- elocute (the dogfood) is stateless — it shouldn't pay for or require a tenant-DB node.
- Lets the platform host apps **with and without** a database, and lets an app **gain a database later** without a recreate.

## Tests / verification
- `app-database-mode.test.ts`: resolver defaults garbage/missing → `none`; reads explicit modes.
- `app-deploy-orchestrator.test.ts`: **isolated** → `ensureTenantDb` called + both DSN vars set; **none** (and default) → no provision, no `DATABASE_URL`, deploy still succeeds.
- **9/9 pass**, 100% line coverage on the new + changed service files; `typecheck` + `biome` clean; route codegen regenerated.

Adds the per-container mutation surface the survival loop needs — the only lifecycle exit today is the billing cron's auto-shutdown.

`v1/containers/[id]/route.ts`:
- **PATCH** `/api/v1/containers/:id` — action-discriminated: `restart` | `setEnv` | `scale`.
- **DELETE** `/api/v1/containers/:id` — `?mode=stop` (preserve the row, billing off) or delete (remove the row); `?purgeVolume=true` to also drop the volume (defaults false).

Each verb maps 1:1 to an existing org-scoped `HetznerContainersClient` method (no new business logic in the route), with `HetznerClientError` → HTTP mapping (`container_not_found`→404, `invalid_input`→400, `ssh_unreachable`/`no_capacity`→503). `DELETE`/stop sets `status=stopped` which halts billing immediately. Router regenerated via codegen.

Verified on the API-only mock: unknown container → 404 `container_not_found`, bad PATCH action → 400, `scale` with desiredCount≠1 → 400 `invalid_input`. Real container mutation runs over SSH to the Docker-on-Hetzner pool (not exercisable on the mock, same as #8258's POST).20:T7e9,## What

`shouldForwardEnv` (the allowlist `buildEnv` uses to decide which host env vars a
spawned ACP coding sub-agent inherits) matched the OS search path as
`key === "PATH"` — case-sensitively.

The repo runtime is Bun, and **Bun on Windows reports the key as `Path`, not
`PATH`**. So `buildEnv` forwarded *none* of it, and every spawned ACP sub-agent
inherited an empty `PATH`. The vendored opencode shim then died at spawn with:

```
'bun' is not recognized as an internal or external command, operable program or batch file.
```

i.e. ACP-driven coding agents were unusable on Windows.

## Fix

- Match the OS system vars **case-insensitively** (a `Set` keyed by uppercase
  name) and broaden the set to the Windows essentials a spawned shell + Bun +
  the agent's config/cache resolution need (`PATHEXT`, `SYSTEMROOT`, `COMSPEC`,
  `SYSTEMDRIVE`, `TEMP`/`TMP`, `USERPROFILE`, `APPDATA`/`LOCALAPPDATA`,
  `PROGRAMFILES`, …) alongside the existing POSIX names.
- **Canonicalize** each forwarded system var to its uppercase form in `buildEnv`
  (all three sources: `process.env`, `customCredentials`, `extra`) so a child
  never inherits two casings of the same var (the winner is undefined on
  Windows) and JS consumers that read `env.PATH` case-sensitively resolve it.
- Extract the projection into the pure, exported `forwardableSubAgentEnv` /
  `canonicalForwardedEnvKey` so the behavior is unit-testable.

The deny-list (`isDeniedSubAgentEnvKey`) still runs **first** (deny wins over
allow), so no secret is newly forwarded — covered by the existing + new tests.

## Testing

- `__tests__/unit/should-forward-env.test.ts` — added Windows-casing,
  no-over-match, system-var, canonicalization, and dual-casing-collapse cases
  (17 pass).
- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator typecheck` clean.

## How it was surfaced

Running the monetized-app economics `/goal` loop end-to-end on Windows: a real
`opencode` ACP sub-agent (Cerebras `gpt-oss-120b`) would not spawn until the
PATH was forwarded.
21:Td0d,## What

Bridges the **already-existing** custom-domain stack into the Product-2 Caddy apps ingress (#8325), so a user's own verified domain (e.g. `elocute.fun`) actually **serves their app** — not just CORS + a DNS target.

### Why this is additive, not a rewrite
A deep codebase scan confirmed custom-domain support already exists and is **reusable** — `managed_domains` (canonical table), the `AppDomains` dashboard (add / copy TXT+CNAME / verify-poll / SSL badges / remove), the buy + DNS-TXT-verify flow, and the `/domains/resolve` resolver. But all of it was **disjoint from the Caddy ingress**: the on-demand-TLS `ask` gate authorized certs only by `containers.public_hostname`, and the per-app route host-matched only that single `<shortid>.apps.elizacloud.ai` host. So a verified custom domain got CORS + a DNS target but **was never served**. This PR is the missing last mile, reusing the existing data + query (no new table, no new query, no UI change).

## Changes (additive, our ingress lane only)

- **`ask` cert-gate** (`v1/apps-ingress/ask/route.ts`): after the `public_hostname` miss, also return `200` when `?domain` is a **verified + active** `managed_domains` row bound to an **active, approved** app — mirroring the reviewed `/api/v1/domains/resolve` predicate. DNS-TXT verification is the ownership proof, so this can't authorize certs for domains the caller doesn't control. The common wildcard path is unchanged and still returns early.
- **route host-match** (`apps-ingress-routes.ts`): `buildCaddyRoute` folds `extraHostnames` (the app's verified custom domains) into the **same** route's host array — deduped + lowercased, same `@id`, so one DELETE tears them all down.
- **provision wiring** (`container-job-executors.ts` + `container-executor-deps.ts`): `executeContainerProvision` gathers the app's verified custom hostnames via a new **injected, best-effort** `listVerifiedAppHostnames` seam (a lookup failure never fails the deploy), wired to the existing `listVerifiedAppOrigins` query. The executor stays schema-decoupled; the whole path is still gated on `APPS_CADDY_ADMIN_URL` (no-op when ingress isn't configured).

- `bun test` (cloud-shared ingress suites): **24/24 pass** — route builder multi-host dedup/lowercase; provision folds verified domains; provision degrades gracefully on a domain-lookup failure; existing single-host + no-ingress paths unchanged. `apps-ingress-routes.ts` at 100% coverage.
- `biome check` clean on all changed files; `typecheck` clean for all changed files (the only diagnostics are pre-existing missing generated i18n files in core/shared).

Wraps the tenant-DB cluster setup in a systemd oneshot service installed via
`write_files`. The service runs on **every boot** (not just the first one),
every step is grep- or path-guarded so it's a no-op when there's nothing to
do.

## Why

After the cpx41/cpx42 rescale this morning (PR #8379), tenant_db came up with
postgres dead. The volume mounted fine but PGDATA never existed:

| Check | Observed |
|---|---|
| `systemctl is-active postgresql@16-main` | `inactive (dead)` |
| `pg_lsclusters` | empty |
| `df -h /mnt/tenant-pgdata` | 32K used (freshly formatted) |
| `ls /mnt/tenant-pgdata/16/main/` | no such file or directory |
| Cloud-init log on rescaled boot | finished in <1s, runcmd absent |

Root cause: cloud-init `runcmd` is per-instance (first boot only). Hetzner
rescale = stop → change type → start, same instance id, so runcmd never
fires again.

## Fixes inside the migration

A few latent bugs in the old `runcmd` script that this version closes:

- fstab append wasn't grep-guarded → duplicate lines on every re-run
- `pg_hba` `hostssl` / `host` lines weren't grep-guarded → same
- `postgresql.conf` seds only matched the commented form (`^#listen_addresses`)
  → silently failed on re-runs after the first sed had uncommented them
- PGDATA check used `! -d` → true for an empty directory left behind by a
  half-finished initdb. Now checks `! -f $PGDATA/PG_VERSION`
- `mkfs.ext4 -F` removed → the `blkid` gate is the only protection; `-F`
  would silently nuke a real fs if `blkid` ever lied
- ALTER USER postgres now waits for `pg_isready` before firing (was racing)
- Script `set -euo pipefail` so partial failures don't masquerade as success

## Workflow input

Adds an optional `replace` input to `terraform-apps-data-plane.yml` —
comma-separated TF resource addresses, threaded as `-replace=<addr>` flags.

Reason: `user_data` lives in `hcloud_server.tenant_db.lifecycle.ignore_changes`
(matches the control-plane convention, `ARCHITECTURE.md` calls this out
explicitly). So a normal apply won't replace the existing tenant_db to pick
up the fixed cloud-init. The `replace` input scratches that itch without
having to taint via local terraform CLI access.

Reusable: any future "rebuild this resource cleanly" follows the same path —
dispatch with `replace="hcloud_server.foo"`, no taint gymnastics.

- [ ] Then load the 2 user DBs from the OLD CP dump (separate, manual `psql -f`)25:Ta88,## Problem

`domains/buy` charged a user's credits for a domain that was **never registered**. On a live run the buy returned `success: true` and debited **$14.95**, but no domain was registered: the returned zone was `stub-zone-<domain>`, the name resolves to a third-party registrar, and `domains/check` still reports it available afterward.

Root cause: `cloudflare-registrar.ts` (and `cloudflare-dns.ts`) have a dev stub gated only by `ELIZA_CF_REGISTRAR_DEV_STUB=1`, documented as "local dev + integration tests ... without spending money on real registrations." When that flag is set in **production**, the stub returns fake successes. The buy route (`v1/apps/[id]/domains/buy/route.ts`) debits credits **before** registering and only refunds when the registrar **throws** (catch -> `refundCredits` -> 502). A stub success never throws, so the refund never fires and the caller is charged for nothing. (`ensureConfigured` already fails correctly when the stub is *off* and creds are missing — the whole failure mode is the stub running in prod.)

## Fix

Make the dev stub **fail closed in production**, matching the existing footgun guards in `deployment-environment.ts` (`shouldBlockDevnetBypass`, `shouldBlockUnsafeWebhookSkip`):

- add `shouldBlockRegistrarStub(env)` = `ELIZA_CF_REGISTRAR_DEV_STUB === "1" && isProductionDeployment(env)`
- `cloudflare-registrar.ts` and `cloudflare-dns.ts` `config()` throw when it returns true

The throw happens per request inside `config()`, which the buy flow hits in `checkAvailability()` **before** any credit debit — so a misconfigured production fails loudly with **no charge** instead of silently faking registrations. Dev/test/staging (`NODE_ENV=test`, `ENVIRONMENT != production`) keep using the stub unchanged, so the cloud-e2e `monetized-app-loop` suite is unaffected.

## Tests

- `deployment-environment.test.ts` — `shouldBlockRegistrarStub` blocks only when the flag is on **and** in production (via `ENVIRONMENT` or `NODE_ENV`), allows dev/test/staging, and is off when the flag is unset.
- `cloudflare-registrar.test.ts` — the registrar **throws** when the stub flag is set with `ENVIRONMENT=production`, and still serves stub data outside production.

`bun test` (cloud-shared): 7/7 pass. Diff is additive only (no reformatting).

## Note for operators

Production should also **unset `ELIZA_CF_REGISTRAR_DEV_STUB`** and configure real `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` / `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`; until real creds exist, domain buys should fail rather than fake-succeed. A latent follow-up (out of scope here): the buy route keeps the debit on an async "pending" registration that later fails — it only refunds on a synchronous throw.26:T94f,## Why

Staging migration today landed a TF-spawned control-plane VM that was unroutable for agent traffic:

- nginx ran the default Ubuntu vhost on :80 (no proxy_pass to agent-router on \`127.0.0.1:3458\`)
- :443 had no listener at all
- CF DNS record \`eliza-staging-1.elizacloud.ai\` was \`proxied=false\` (grey cloud), so CF Workers fetching it went direct → got 521 on missing :443

Repaired manually on the live VM. This PR bakes the same setup into the TF module so the next apply (prod, or any re-spawn) lands a routable VM out of the box.

## What

**cloud-init/bootstrap.yaml.tftpl**:
- New \`/etc/nginx/sites-available/eliza-agent-router\` vhost (mirrors prod CP setup) — \`*.elizacloud.ai → 127.0.0.1:3458\` on :80 + :443
- Generate self-signed \`*.elizacloud.ai\` cert at boot via \`openssl req -x509\` → \`/etc/nginx/ssl/eliza-agent-router.{crt,key}\`
- Drop the default vhost + enable the new one + reload nginx
- Git clone retries 3× with 5s sleep — silent race with apt-installed \`git\` left /opt/eliza empty on a slow image

**main.tf**:
- \`cloudflare_dns_record.control_plane.proxied = true\` + \`ttl = 1\` (CF API requires Auto TTL when proxied)
- Comment explains why direct origin fetch fails on self-signed cert

## Why self-signed is fine

Zone SSL on \`elizacloud.ai\` is set to "Full" (not "Full (Strict)"). CF accepts any cert on origin when terminating TLS with the visitor. The self-signed cert is regenerated on every boot — no expiry to manage, no key rotation.

## Side effect

SSH to \`eliza-${env}-N.elizacloud.ai\` now resolves to CF IPs (proxied A record blocks the origin from DNS). Ops must SSH by **public IP** from terraform output \`control_plane_vms.<idx>.ipv4\`, not by hostname. Same pattern prod \`eliza-1\` already uses since it's also proxied in the live CF zone.

  - \`curl -I https://<vm-ip>/\` returns 404 (= reaches agent-router which 404s on non-agent Host) — not 521 / connection refused

  - DNS record \`eliza-<env>-1.elizacloud.ai\` is proxied=true in Cloudflare27:T783,## Bug

EventSource (the browser SSE client) cannot set request headers, so it has no way to send `Authorization: Bearer <ELIZA_API_TOKEN>`. The global auth gate in `server.ts` only inspects headers via `extractAuthToken(req)`, so every SSE handshake from the cloud web UI 401s before the route handler runs. Local-inference and computer-use stream endpoints inherit the same gate. The token itself is correct (same `agent_<uuid>` value the REST chat path accepts as a Bearer) -- only the transport is wrong.

## Fix

Extend `extractAuthToken()` with a tightly-scoped SSE query-token fallback, mirroring the existing `ELIZA_ALLOW_WS_QUERY_TOKEN=1` path that already ships for WebSockets. The fallback is gated on:

- `ELIZA_ALLOW_WS_QUERY_TOKEN=1` (cloud-only opt-in, no regression elsewhere)
- `method === GET` (read-only)
- `Accept` header contains `text/event-stream` (scope strictly to SSE so a stray `?token=` on a JSON endpoint cannot bypass the header path or leak via proxy access logs)
- Token length <= 1024 (mirrors the header cap)

Validation still goes through `tokenMatches()` -> `crypto.timingSafeEqual`, identical to the Bearer path -- no new comparison semantics.

## Test plan

- [x] `bunx vitest run test/api/server-helpers-auth.test.ts` -> 21/21 pass, including 6 new SSE cases:
  - `?token=<good>` + `Accept: text/event-stream` + flag on -> authorized
  - `?token=<wrong>` + same setup -> 401
  - `?token=<good>` but flag unset -> 401 (non-cloud deploys stay locked)
  - `?token=<good>` but `Accept: application/json` -> 401 (SSE-scope only)
  - POST + SSE Accept + `?token=<good>` -> 401 (read-only safety)
  - Bearer takes precedence over `?token=` when both present
- [x] `bunx tsgo --noEmit` on packages/agent: clean for changed file
- [ ] Manual: with `ELIZA_ALLOW_WS_QUERY_TOKEN=1` + `ELIZA_API_TOKEN` set, hit `/api/conversations/:id/messages/stream?token=<api-token>` via EventSource on staging28:T716,## What

Adds an **encryption-free** path for the Apps (Product 2) deploy backend so the
provisioning daemon can provision per-tenant databases **without a `SECRETS_MASTER_KEY`**.

The daemon runs `ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND=memory` with **no** `SECRETS_MASTER_KEY`, so it does
zero field-encryption today. The apps flow only used encryption to (a) decrypt the cluster
admin DSN and (b) store the per-tenant URI in `app_databases` — both avoidable:

- **`makeDirectAppDeployRunner`** (new, in `app-deploy-runner.ts`) — provisions the tenant DB
  directly via `TenantDbProvisioning` and skips the encrypted `app_databases` write.
- **`configureAppsDeployBackend`** branches on `APPS_TENANT_ADMIN_DSN`: when set, the cluster
  admin DSN is sourced from env (passthrough decrypt) and the direct runner is used; otherwise
  it falls back to the standard encrypted `UserDatabaseService` path **unchanged**.

This is **seamless** with the eventual CI/secrets route: the moment `SECRETS_MASTER_KEY` +
encrypted cluster rows exist, drop `APPS_TENANT_ADMIN_DSN` and the encrypted path takes over
with no code change.

- Real `makeTenantDbProvisioning` → `provisionForApp` against the staging Postgres: two tenants
  provisioned, each reaches its **own** DB, cross-tenant `permission denied for database` (REVOKE CONNECT).
- Full e2e on staging: real sample-app containers serve their URL while reaching **only** their own
  isolated tenant DB; cross-tenant rejected through a real container; real `--internal` network
  builder blocks egress (incl. public IPs).
- `bun test` app-lane: 21/21 pass. Biome clean. Typecheck clean.

## Safety

Additive + feature-gated. With `APPS_TENANT_ADMIN_DSN` unset (default), behavior is identical to today.

2c:T826,## What

Arms **build-from-repo** on the apps daemon — the "Vercel-like" path where the **platform builds the user's repo**, instead of requiring a manually pre-pushed image.

## Why

The full pipeline (buildx-over-SSH → push → pull → deploy) was already coded + unit-tested, but `configureAppsDeployBackend` was called with no `buildExec`, so the daemon armed **prebuilt-only**: `app.github_repo` was read but went nowhere, and every deploy needed a hand-built, pre-pushed image. That's the exact un-Vercel-like friction — the registry push is supposed to be *invisible plumbing the platform does*, not a manual prerequisite.

## How

- `makeNodeBuilderExec()` (container-executor-deps) exposes the **app node's own SSH** as a `BuildExec` — the node already has Docker + buildx (it runs the containers), and `AppContainerSsh.exec(cmd, timeoutMs)` is structurally identical to `BuildExec.exec`. Returns `null` when the container backend isn't configured.
- `configureAppsDeployBackend`: when a **registry** is configured (`APPS_IMAGE_REGISTRY` env or `config.registry`), it builds the user's repo on the node via buildx and pushes; the node then pulls + runs it.
- The daemon passes `registry: process.env.APPS_IMAGE_REGISTRY` at arm time.

## Safety

**Fully gated.** With `APPS_IMAGE_REGISTRY` **unset** (today's state), behavior is identical to before — prebuilt images (`imageTag`/`APP_DEFAULT_IMAGE`). No risk to the live path. 4 new tests pin the arming gate; typecheck + biome clean.

## Infra to actually turn it on (follow-up, needs @standujar)

The app node needs **buildx** + `docker login <registry>` (push for build, pull for run) — same out-of-band provisioning as the container SSH key. Then set `APPS_IMAGE_REGISTRY` (e.g. `ghcr.io/elizaos`) on the daemon and re-arm.

The remaining product gap after this is the browser **"import from GitHub"** UX (the dashboard create flow only registers externally-hosted apps today).

Found via a full vision-vs-reality gap scan of Product 2 — this was the **#1 gap** (the literal headline feature). cc @standujar
